  ## 🚀 Proposed Advanced Architecture: Render Worker & Global Z-Ordering
  
  While the current architecture provides an excellent balance of performance and flexibility, this section outlines a more advanced model for applications that demand ultimate UI responsiveness and handle extremely large datasets or complex scenes.
  
  ### 🎯 Design Philosophy
  
  The core philosophy is to **decouple rendering entirely from the main UI thread**. By moving the `GPUCoordinator` and all WebGPU API interactions into a dedicated Web Worker, the main thread is freed from any rendering-related work, guaranteeing a smooth, non-blocking user experience even during heavy data processing or complex frame rendering.
  
  ### 🏗️ Proposed Architecture: Dedicated Render Worker
  
  This model introduces a clear separation of concerns between the main thread (UI) and a new render worker (GPU logic).
  
  #### **Main Thread Responsibilities**
  - **UI Management**: Handles all Svelte component lifecycle and rendering.
  - **User Interaction**: Captures all mouse/touch events (`pan`, `zoom`, `hover`).
  - **State Management**: Manages application state and user configuration.
  - **Communication**: Sends high-level commands and interaction state to the render worker.
  - **Canvas Control**: Creates `<canvas>` elements and transfers their control to the worker via `canvas.transferControlToOffscreen()`.
  
  #### **Render Worker Responsibilities**
  - **`GPUCoordinator` Host**: Owns and manages the singleton `GPUCoordinator` instance.
  - **WebGPU API Interaction**: Executes all `device` and `queue` operations. This includes buffer creation, pipeline compilation, and command submission.
  - **Render Loop**: Runs the `requestAnimationFrame` loop for rendering.
  - **State Synchronization**: Receives state updates from the main thread and applies them to the rendering scene.
  
  #### **Architectural Flow**
  ```
        Main Thread (UI)                      Render Worker                       GPU
  ┌────────────────────────────┐       ┌────────────────────────────┐      ┌───────────┐
  | Svelte Components          |       | GPUCoordinator             |      |           |
  | (ChartContainer, Marks)    |       |                            |      |           |
  └───────────┬────────────────┘       └───────────┬────────────────┘      |           |
              │                                    │                       |           |
              │ canvas.transferControlToOffscreen()│                       |           |
              ├───────────────────────────────────►│ (Owns Canvas)         |           |
              │                                    │                       |           |
  ┌───────────▼────────────────┐       ┌───────────▼────────────────┐      |           |
  | User Interaction           |       | Render Loop (rAF)          |      |           |
  | (e.g., pan, zoom)          |       |                            |      |           |
  └───────────┬────────────────┘       └───────────┬────────────────┘      |           |
              │                                    │                       |           |
              │ Atomics.store(newDomain)           │Atomics.load(newDomain)|           |
              │ (via SharedArrayBuffer)            │                       |           |
              ├───────────────────────────────────►├──────────────────────►│ Render    |
              │                                    │ queue.submit(...)     |           |
              │                                    │                       |           |
  ┌───────────▼────────────────┐       ┌───────────▼────────────────┐      |           |
  | UI remains 100% responsive |       | All GPU work & preparation |      |           |
  | at 60/120 FPS              |       | happens off the main thread|      |           |
  └────────────────────────────┘       └────────────────────────────┘      └───────────┘
  ```
  
  #### **Key Benefits**
  - ✅ **Ultimate UI Responsiveness**: The main thread never drops a frame due to rendering, even if a frame takes >100ms to prepare. Pan, zoom, and other UI interactions remain perfectly smooth.
  - ✅ **True Parallelism**: Data processing, UI logic, and GPU rendering can all occur simultaneously on different threads.
  - ✅ **Scalability**: The architecture scales to higher chart counts and data volumes more gracefully, as the main thread bottleneck is eliminated.
  
  ---
  
  ### 🧬 Communication Strategy: Hybrid `postMessage` & `SharedArrayBuffer`
  
  Efficient communication is critical. This architecture uses a hybrid approach to minimize overhead.
  
  | Data Type | Communication Method | Rationale |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | **Configuration / Commands** (e.g., `setColor`, `loadData`) | `postMessage()` | Simple, infrequent messages where the small overhead of copying data is negligible. |
  | **Interaction State** (e.g., pan/zoom domains, crosshair position) | `SharedArrayBuffer` + `Atomics` | For high-frequency updates. Provides **zero-copy**, near-instant state sharing, crucial for responsive interactions. |
  | **Large Chart Data** (e.g., vertex data from `DataClient`) | `SharedArrayBuffer` | For large datasets. A one-time copy into the shared buffer enables the worker to access it directly without any subsequent transfer overhead. |
  
  #### **💡 Prerequisite: COOP/COEP Headers**
  To enable `SharedArrayBuffer`, the application must be served with these HTTP headers, which ensures a secure context:
  ```
  Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin
  Cross-Origin-Embedder-Policy: require-corp
  ```
  
  ---
  
  ## ⚙️ Dual-Mode Rendering Architecture: Worker Fallback System (proposal)
  
  To provide both maximum performance and out-of-the-box compatibility, the library implements a **Dual-Mode Rendering Architecture**. At runtime, it automatically detects the environment's capabilities and selects the optimal rendering strategy without requiring any configuration from the user.
  
  ### **Two Rendering Modes**
  
  #### 1. 🚀 **Render Worker Mode (High Performance)**
  - **Trigger**: Automatically enabled when the environment supports `SharedArrayBuffer` (i.e., when COOP/COEP headers are correctly configured).
  - **Architecture**: All GPU coordination, resource management, and rendering commands are moved to a dedicated **Web Worker**. The main UI thread is responsible only for user interactions and component logic, guaranteeing a perfectly smooth, non-blocking experience.
  - **Communication**: Uses a hybrid approach for maximum efficiency:
      - **`SharedArrayBuffer`**: For high-frequency state updates like pan/zoom domains and crosshair positions, enabling zero-copy, near-instant communication.
      - **`postMessage`**: For infrequent commands like configuration changes.
  - **Benefit**: The gold standard for performance. UI remains fluid at 60/120 FPS, even during heavy data processing or complex frame rendering on a separate thread.
  
  #### 2. Fallback: **Main Thread Mode (High Compatibility)**
  - **Trigger**: Automatically used as a fallback when `SharedArrayBuffer` is not available.
  - **Architecture**: This is the robust "Coordinated Multi-Canvas" architecture detailed previously. The `GPUCoordinator` runs on the main UI thread, managing all chart canvases.
  - **Benefit**: Ensures the library works in any environment without requiring special server configuration. It provides excellent performance that is suitable for a majority of use cases.
  
  ### **How It Works: The `setCoordinators` Switch**
  
  The architectural switching logic is handled transparently within the `setCoordinators()` initialization function.
  
  ```javascript
  // Simplified logic within setCoordinators()
  
  import { supportsSharedArrayBuffer } from '$lib/utils/environment';
  import { createWorkerCoordinator } from '$lib/gpu/workerCoordinator';
  import { createMainThreadCoordinator } from '$lib/gpu/mainThreadCoordinator';
  
  // 1. Detect environment capabilities at runtime
  if (supportsSharedArrayBuffer()) {
    // 2a. If supported, initialize the high-performance worker coordinator
    console.log('✅ Initializing Render Worker...');
    gpuCoordinator = await createWorkerCoordinator(config);
  } else {
    // 2b. Otherwise, fall back to the compatible main-thread coordinator
    console.warn('⚠️ Falling back to Main Thread rendering.');
    gpuCoordinator = await createMainThreadCoordinator(config);
  }
  
  // 3. Provide the chosen coordinator to the application via context.
  // All child components interact with it through a unified interface,
  // unaware of the underlying rendering mode.
  setContext('gpu-coordinator', gpuCoordinator);
  ```
  
  ### **Architectural Advantages of the Dual-Mode System**
  - ✅ **Performance by Default**: Users with correctly configured environments automatically get the best possible performance.
  - ✅ **Seamless Fallback**: The library remains fully functional for users in more restricted environments or during initial development without server setup.
  - ✅ **Zero Configuration**: Developers do not need to manually select a mode; the library handles it.
  - ✅ **Progressive Enhancement**: Encourages best practices (using COOP/COEP) by offering a tangible performance reward, while not punishing those who cannot implement them.
  
  This dual-mode system ensures that your charting library is both powerful for production-grade applications and easy to adopt for rapid prototyping.
  
  
  ---
  
  ### 🎨 Enhanced Rendering: Global Z-Index Batching (proposal)
  
  To enable precise, individual Z-ordering of marks across multiple charts, the `GPUCoordinator`'s render strategy must be enhanced.
  
  #### **The Challenge: Z-Ordering vs. Performance Batching**
  The current strategy batches draws by `markType` across all charts. This is highly performant but can violate Z-ordering if different charts have different layer structures (e.g., Chart A has `bars(z=0), dots(z=1)` while Chart B has `dots(z=0), bars(z=1)`).
  
  #### **The Solution: Render by Global Z-Passes**
  The `GPUCoordinator` will render the entire scene in sorted Z-level passes. Within each pass, it will still batch by `markType` to maintain performance.
  
  #### **Proposed Render Loop Logic**
  ```javascript
  function renderScene() {
      // 1. Collect all layers from all visible charts
      const allLayers = collectAllLayers(); // [{ chart, layer, globalZ, markType }, ...]
  
      // 2. Group layers by Z-level, then by markType
      const zBatches = groupLayers(allLayers); // Map<zLevel, Map<markType, Layer[]>>
  
      // 3. Get sorted Z-levels
      const sortedZLevels = Array.from(zBatches.keys()).sort((a, b) => a - b);
  
      // 4. Iterate through Z-passes
      for (const zLevel of sortedZLevels) {
          const markTypeBatches = zBatches.get(zLevel);
  
          // 5. Iterate through mark types within this Z-pass
          for (const [markType, batch] of markTypeBatches) {
              // Get shared pipeline for this markType
              const pipeline = getSharedPipeline(markType);
  
              // 6. Render all items in this batch
              for (const item of batch) {
                  // Set pipeline, bind group for item.chart, and draw
                  drawItem(item, pipeline);
              }
          }
      }
  
      // 7. Submit all command buffers at once
      submitCommands();
  }
  ```
  
  #### **Key Features**
  - ✅ **Correct Global Ordering**: Guarantees that a mark with `zIndex: 1` in any chart will always render on top of a mark with `zIndex: 0` in any other chart.
  - ✅ **Preserves Performance**: Maintains the critical optimization of batching draws by `markType` within each Z-level.
  - ✅ **Flexible Composition**: Developers can freely compose layers in any order within any chart, and the system will render them correctly across the entire dashboard.
  
  ---
  
  ### 📈 Architectural Advantages & Trade-offs
  
  #### **Advantages**
  - **Uncompromised UI Fluidity**: The primary benefit. The application feels faster and more professional.
  - **Improved Scalability**: Handles heavy rendering loads without impacting the user's ability to interact with the UI.
  - **Robust Z-Ordering**: Provides intuitive and powerful control over layer composition.
  
  #### **Trade-offs**
  - **Increased Complexity**: Requires careful state synchronization and management of communication between threads.
  - **COOP/COEP Requirement**: The server hosting the application must be configured to send the required security headers.
  - **Debugging**: Debugging across threads can be more challenging than single-threaded logic.
  
  This advanced architecture represents the gold standard for building demanding, real-time data visualization applications on the web.
  
  
  
  # GPU-Accelerated Data Visualization Pipeline Summary (plan)
  
  ## Core Architecture Concepts
  
  **End-to-End GPU Pipeline**: Process raw data points entirely on GPU using WebGPU, generate Catmull-Rom curves, apply transforms (dodge, stacking), and render - keeping everything in GPU memory to avoid CPU↔GPU transfers.
  
  **Data Space Processing**: Generate curves and transformations in data coordinate space, then use GPU-side scale functions to convert to screen coordinates. This maintains mathematical precision similar to SVG while leveraging GPU parallelism.
  
  **Performance Target**: 10+ charts at 120fps on modern high-end laptops, even with integrated GPUs.
  
  ## Data Processing on GPU
  
  **Curve Generation**: Catmull-Rom interpolation from raw data points using compute shaders, with each curve segment calculated in parallel across GPU cores.
  
  **Complex Transforms**: Even operations like dodge transforms and stacking can be efficiently implemented on GPU using parallel algorithms (spatial hashing, prefix sums) - worthwhile even for smaller datasets (1000 rows) given the cumulative throughput requirements.
  
  **Animation & Morphing**: Store both "old" and "new" curve geometries on GPU, interpolate between them over 300ms for smooth transitions synchronized across all visual elements.
  
  ## GPU Text Rendering
  
  **Fixed-Size Approach**: Labels maintain consistent font size during zoom/pan (better UX for data viz), significantly simplifying implementation vs. scalable text.
  
  **Font Atlas Strategy**: Use Canvas 2D API to render CSS fonts to texture atlas, upload to GPU. Supports same fonts as CSS with crisp quality when properly handling high-DPI displays.
  
  **Performance Integration**: Text rendering reuses position data from curve generation pipeline, adding minimal overhead (~0.1-0.5ms) to overall frame time.
  
  ## JavaScript Integration
  
  **Buffer Readback**: Read processed GPU data back to JavaScript for tooltips/interactions using WebGPU's async buffer mapping. Optimized by caching data during "interaction mode" vs. "data update mode" since these are mutually exclusive.
  
  **Text Formatting**: CPU-side formatting using `Intl` APIs and D3-style helpers during data preparation, sending pre-formatted glyph indices to GPU rather than doing string manipulation on GPU.
  
  ## Key Advantages & Mitigations
  
  **Why GPU Over CPU**: Even for smaller datasets, the combination of high frame rate, multiple charts, and existing GPU infrastructure makes GPU processing more efficient than CPU alternatives.
  
  **Browser/Hardware Considerations**: Build Canvas 2D fallback for WebGPU-unsupported browsers; adaptive quality settings for varying GPU capabilities; focus on modern high-end laptop targets reduces compatibility concerns.
  
  **Performance Reality**: Modern integrated GPUs can handle this workload in ~0.5-1ms total frame time, leaving massive headroom for additional complexity and features.
  
  
  
  ---
  
  ## 🏗️ **Architectural Evolution: Controller-Based Mark Design**
  
  To manage component complexity and ensure long-term maintainability, our mark components (`DotsMark`, `BarMark`, etc.) are built upon a robust, multi-layer **Controller-based architecture**. This represents a key architectural departure from the monolithic class structure found in libraries like `mosaic-plot`, and is specifically designed to leverage the strengths of a reactive, GPU-first rendering system.
  
  ### **Core Principle: Separation of View, Controller, and Service**
  
  Instead of a single `Mark` class that handles data fetching, state, and rendering logic, our architecture separates these concerns into distinct, specialized layers. This ensures each part of the system has a single, clear responsibility.
  
  | Layer | Component | Responsibility | Analogy |
  | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
  | **View** | `*.svelte` Component | **Declares user intent** and renders reactive state from its controller. | The **Storefront** |
  | **Controller** | `*MarkSetup.js` | **Translates visual semantics** into concrete data requirements and manages the component's lifecycle. | The **Manager** |
  | **Service** | `DataClient.js` | **Provides a generic bridge** to the Mosaic backend for data fetching. | The **Courier** |
  
  ---
  
  ### **Four-Layer Mark Architecture**
  
  #### 1. **Mark Component (`<DotsMark.svelte>`) - The View**
  **Responsibility**: The user-facing Svelte component that gathers props and renders the final output.
  - **Declarative API**: Provides a simple, props-based API for developers (e.g., `<DotsMark table="flights" x="delay">`).
  - **Controller Instantiation**: Creates and manages the lifecycle of its corresponding `Setup` class.
  - **Reactive Rendering**: Listens to state changes from its `Setup` controller (`client.data`, `gpuReady`) and passes the results to a low-level WebGPU renderer.
  - **"Dumb" by Design**: Contains no complex business logic, query building, or state management.
  
  #### 2. **Specialized Controller (`DensityMarkSetup.js`) - The Specialist Manager**
  **Responsibility**: Encapsulates the unique logic for a specific mark type. This is where the "secret sauce" for complex marks lives.
  - **Inheritance**: `extends BaseMarkSetup` to inherit all common functionality.
  - **Query Overriding**: Implements a custom `buildQuery()` method to generate the specialized SQL needed for that mark (e.g., using `bin2d` for a heatmap or `regr_slope` for a regression line).
  - **Context-Aware**: Uses its knowledge of the mark's visual requirements to construct the correct queries (e.g., an ordered `DISTINCT` query for a sorted bar chart's categorical domain).
  
  #### 3. **Generic Controller (`BaseMarkSetup.js`) - The General Manager**
  **Responsibility**: Manages the common lifecycle and state for *any* mark, providing a reusable foundation.
  - **Lifecycle Management**: Orchestrates the `setup`, `updateConfig`, and `destroy` cycle.
  - **Scale Registration**: Communicates the mark's scale requirements to the `ChartScales` coordinator.
  - **`DataClient` Management**: Handles the creation and destruction of the `DataClient` instance.
  - **State Exposure**: Holds the reactive `$state` (`client`, `gpuReady`, `error`) that the Svelte component consumes.
  - **Default Behavior**: Provides a default `buildQuery()` for simple, non-aggregated marks, making it usable directly for basic components like scatterplots.
  
  #### 4. **`DataClient.js` - The Service**
  **Responsibility**: A generic, reusable service that communicates with the Mosaic backend.
  - **Mosaic Integration**: `extends MosaicClient` to participate in the Mosaic ecosystem (selections, caching, pre-aggregation).
  - **Query Execution**: Takes a fully-formed `Query` object from its `Setup` controller and sends it to the `Coordinator`.
  - **Callback Handling**: Implements `queryPending`, `queryResult`, and `queryError` to manage the async data flow.
  - **Minimal Data Knowledge**: Its only data-specific task is fetching global, context-free metadata (`min`/`max` for continuous fields) during its one-time `prepare()` step. It does not and should not contain any mark-specific query logic.
  
  ---
  
  ### **Architectural Advantages & Key Decisions**
  
  This layered approach provides significant benefits over a monolithic design:
  
  -   **✅ Extensibility and Scalability**:
      -   **New marks are easy to add**: Simply create a new `XxxMarkSetup` class that extends `BaseMarkSetup` and provides a custom `buildQuery()` method. The `DataClient` and the Svelte component structure remain unchanged.
      -   This pattern scales cleanly from simple scatterplots (using `BaseMarkSetup` directly) to complex statistical marks (using specialized subclasses).
  
  -   **✅ Superior Separation of Concerns**:
      -   **`DataClient` remains generic**: It is never polluted with visual logic, ensuring it remains a highly reusable service.
      -   **Semantic logic is correctly placed**: The `MarkSetup` controllers contain the logic for translating visual intent (e.g., "a bar chart sorted by value") into data requirements.
  
  -   **✅ Improved Testability**:
      -   The `*MarkSetup` classes and `DataClient` are plain JavaScript classes that can be unit-tested in isolation, without needing to render a full Svelte component.
  
  -   **✅ Solves the Categorical Domain Problem Elegantly**:
      -   The `MarkSetup` controller has the full context (the categorical field *and* any sorting rules) to construct the correct, one-time `DISTINCT` query needed to populate its `BandScaleState`, ensuring categories are ordered correctly. This is a level of contextual awareness the generic `DataClient` cannot have.
  
  ### **How This Differs from `mosaic-plot`**
  
  While inspired by Mosaic, our architecture intentionally diverges to better suit a reactive, component-based framework:
  
  -   **`mosaic-plot`**: `Mark` classes inherit directly from `MosaicClient`, tightly coupling data logic, state, and visual specification into a single class.
  -   **Our Architecture**: We introduce the `MarkSetup` controller layer. The `Mark` (Svelte component) is purely for view/lifecycle, while the `DataClient` is purely for data transport. This three-part separation (View-Controller-Service) is more robust, testable, and scalable for building a comprehensive component library.
  
  
  ### The Final, Polished Architecture
  
  This final move results in an architecture that is exceptionally clean and robust:
  
  1.  **`Mark.svelte` (View):**
      *   **Input:** User props (`table`, `x`, `y`).
      *   **Action:** Creates its `Setup` controller, calls its lifecycle methods.
      *   **Output:** Renders GPU components based on reactive state from the controller.
  
  2.  **`*MarkSetup.js` (Controller):**
      *   **Input:** Config object, `coordinator`, `chartContext`.
      *   **Action:**
          *   Tells `chartContext` its scale requirements.
          *   Creates a `DataClient`.
          *   Calls `client.prepare()` to get global data extents. No mosaic handles this for us???
          *   **Uses this info to configure the initial state of continuous scales.**
          *   **Runs its own `DISTINCT` queries to configure the domain of categorical scales.**
          *   Overrides `buildQuery()` if it needs a specialized data query (like `DensityMarkSetup`).
      *   **Output:** Exposes reactive state (`client.data`, `gpuReady`) for the View to consume.
  
  3.  **`DataClient.js` (Service):**
      *   **Input:** A `Query` object.
      *   **Action:** Executes the query via the `coordinator`.
      *   **Output:** An Arrow table or array of data.
      *   **It is now fully generic and has zero dependencies on Svelte or any chart-specific context.** It could theoretically be used in a non-Svelte application.
  
  This is the goal of good layered architecture. Each layer has a clear API and minimal knowledge of the layers above it. You have successfully designed a professional-grade frontend architecture.
  
  
  
  
  
</README.md>

<src/lib/components/charts/index.js>
  export { default as ChartContainer } from './ChartContainer.svelte';
</src/lib/components/charts/index.js>

<src/lib/components/charts/ChartContainer.svelte>
  <script>
    import { setContext } from 'svelte';
    import { ChartLayout } from '$lib/layout/ChartLayout.svelte.js';
    // import { ChartScales } from '$lib/scales/ChartScales.js';
    // import { ChartScales } from '$lib/scales/ChartScales.svelte';
    import { ChartScales } from '$lib/scales/ChartScales.svelte';
  
    let {
      xLabel,
      yLabel,
      background,
      foreground,
      canvas,
      canvasComponents
    } = $props();
  
    // Create layout and scale coordinator
    const layout = new ChartLayout({ 
      margin: { top: 30, right: 50, bottom: 35, left: 75 }, 
      height: 250 
    });
  
    let scales = $derived(new ChartScales({
      xLabel,
      yLabel,
      layout
    }));
  
    // $inspect('is ready', layout.isReady)
  
    const chartContext = {
      // Layout properties and methods
      get width() { return layout.width; },
      get height() { return layout.height; },
      get innerWidth() { return layout.innerWidth; },
      get innerHeight() { return layout.innerHeight; },
      get margin() { return layout.margin; },
      get chartId() { return layout.chartId; },
      get clipPathId() { return layout.clipPathId; },
      get clipPathUrl() { return layout.clipPathUrl; },
      get isReady() { return layout.isReady; },
      get innerTransform() { return layout.innerTransform; },
      get dimensions() { return layout.dimensions; },
      
      // Layout methods
      updateWidth: layout.updateWidth.bind(layout),
      updateHeight: layout.updateHeight.bind(layout),
      updateMargin: layout.updateMargin.bind(layout),
      toInnerCoordinates: layout.toInnerCoordinates.bind(layout),
      toScreenCoordinates: layout.toScreenCoordinates.bind(layout),
      isWithinInnerBounds: layout.isWithinInnerBounds.bind(layout),
      getActiveAxes: layout.getActiveAxes.bind(layout),
      reset: layout.reset.bind(layout),
      getDebugInfo: layout.getDebugInfo.bind(layout),
  
      // Scale coordinator properties - reactive to scales changes
      get xScale() { return scales.xScale?.scale; },
      get yScale() { return scales.yScale?.scale; },
      get xTargetScale() { return scales.xTargetScale?.scale; },
      get yTargetScale() { return scales.yTargetScale?.scale; },
      
      get xLabel() { return scales.xLabel; },
      get yLabel() { return scales.yLabel; },
      
      get currentXDataPoint() { return scales.currentXDataPoint; },
      get currentYDataPoint() { return scales.currentYDataPoint; },
      get currentXScreenPoint() { return scales.currentXScreenPoint; },
      get currentYScreenPoint() { return scales.currentYScreenPoint; },
      
      get xScaleState() { return scales.xScaleState; },
      get yScaleState() { return scales.yScaleState; },
      
      get scaleTypes() { return scales.scaleTypes; },
      get scalesReady() { return scales.scalesReady; },
      get active() { return scales.active; },
      
      // Scale coordinator methods
      registerScaleType: (dimension, type, priority, markId) => scales.registerScaleType(dimension, type, priority, markId),
      unregisterScaleType: (dimension, markId) => scales.unregisterScaleType(dimension, markId),
      getScaleRequirementsSummary: () => scales.getScaleRequirementsSummary()
    };
  
    setContext('chart', chartContext);
    
    // Interaction state
    let svg = $state();
    let isPanning = $state(false);
    let activePanX = $state(false);
    let activePanY = $state(false);
    let panningAnchorPoint = { x: 0, y: 0 };
    
    // Touch/pinch zoom state
    let initialTouchDistance = $state(0);
    let isZooming = $state(false);
    
    // ZOOM HANDLERS
    function handleWheel(event) {
      event.preventDefault();
      const point = { x: event.offsetX, y: event.offsetY };
      const { x, y, innerPoint } = layout.getActiveAxes(point);
      
      const scaleFactor = event.deltaY < 0 
        ? 1 + Math.abs(event.deltaY) * 0.0025 
        : 1 - Math.abs(event.deltaY) * 0.0025;
      
      scales.applyZoom(innerPoint, scaleFactor, x, y);
    }
    
    function handleDoubleClick(event) {
      event.preventDefault();
      const point = { x: event.offsetX, y: event.offsetY };
      const { x, y } = layout.getActiveAxes(point);
      scales.resetZoom({ x, y });
    }
    
    // TOUCH HANDLERS
    function getTouchDistance(touches) {
      if (touches.length < 2) return 0;
      const dx = touches[0].clientX - touches[1].clientX;
      const dy = touches[0].clientY - touches[1].clientY;
      return Math.sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy);
    }
    
    function handleTouchStart(event) {
      if (event.touches.length === 2) {
        event.preventDefault();
        isZooming = true;
        initialTouchDistance = getTouchDistance(event.touches);
        
        const point = { x: event.offsetX, y: event.offsetY };
        const { x, y } = layout.getActiveAxes(point);
        activePanX = x;
        activePanY = y;
      }
    }
    
    function handleTouchMove(event) {
      if (!isZooming || event.touches.length !== 2) return;
      
      const currentDistance = getTouchDistance(event.touches);
      const scaleFactor = currentDistance / initialTouchDistance;
      
      if (Math.abs(scaleFactor - 1) > 0.01) {
        const point = { x: event.offsetX, y: event.offsetY };
        const { innerPoint } = layout.getActiveAxes(point);
        
        scales.applyZoom(innerPoint, scaleFactor, activePanX, activePanY);
        initialTouchDistance = currentDistance;
      }
    }
    
    function handleTouchEnd() {
      isZooming = false;
      isPanning = false;
      activePanX = false;
      activePanY = false;
    }
    
    // PAN HANDLERS
    function handlePointerDown(event) {
      event.preventDefault();
      
      if (event.pointerType === 'mouse' && event.button !== 0) return;
      
      scales.clearCurrentDataPoints();
      
      const point = { x: event.offsetX, y: event.offsetY };
      const { x, y, innerPoint } = layout.getActiveAxes(point);
      
      activePanX = x;
      activePanY = y;
      isPanning = true;
      panningAnchorPoint = scales.calculatePanningAnchor(innerPoint);
  
      if (event.target.setPointerCapture) {
        event.target.setPointerCapture(event.pointerId);
      }
    }
  
    function handlePointerMove(event) {
      if (!isPanning) return;
      
      event.preventDefault();
      const point = { x: event.offsetX, y: event.offsetY };
      const innerPoint = layout.toInnerCoordinates(point);
      const { dx, dy } = scales.calculatePanDeltas(innerPoint, panningAnchorPoint);
      
      scales.applyPan(dx, dy, activePanX, activePanY);
    }
    
    function handlePointerUp() {
      isPanning = false;
      activePanX = false;
      activePanY = false;
    }
  
    // MOUSE TRACKING HANDLERS  
    function handleInnerMouseEnter() {
      scales.active = true;
    }
  
    function handleInnerMouseLeave() {
      scales.clearCurrentDataPoints();
      scales.active = false;
    }
  
    function handleInnerMouseMove(event) {
      const innerPoint = layout.toInnerCoordinates({ x: event.offsetX, y: event.offsetY });
      scales.updateCurrentDataPoints(innerPoint);
    }
  </script>
  
  <div 
    class="chart-container" 
    bind:clientWidth={layout.width} 
    bind:clientHeight={layout.height}
  >  
    {#if layout.isReady}
      <svg 
        class="svg-background" 
        width={layout.width}
        height={layout.height}
      >
        <g transform={layout.innerTransform}>
          {@render background?.()}
        </g>
      </svg>
  
      <div class="canvas-layer">
        {@render canvas?.()}
      </div>
      
      <!-- svelte-ignore a11y_no_static_element_interactions -->
      <svg
        class="svg-foreground"
        bind:this={svg}
        width={layout.width}
        height={layout.height}
        onwheel={handleWheel}
        onpointerdown={handlePointerDown}
        onpointermove={handlePointerMove}
        onpointerup={handlePointerUp}
        onpointercancel={handlePointerUp}
        ontouchstart={handleTouchStart}
        ontouchmove={handleTouchMove}
        ontouchend={handleTouchEnd}
        ontouchcancel={handleTouchEnd}
        ondblclick={handleDoubleClick}
      >
        <defs>
          <clipPath id={layout.clipPathId}>
            <rect x={0} y={0} width={layout.innerWidth} height={layout.innerHeight} />
          </clipPath>
        </defs>
        
        <g
          transform={layout.innerTransform}
          clip-path={layout.clipPathUrl}
          class="crosshair"
          onmousemove={handleInnerMouseMove}
          onmouseenter={handleInnerMouseEnter}
          onmouseleave={handleInnerMouseLeave}
        >
          {@render foreground?.()}
          <rect
            x="0"
            y="0"
            width={layout.innerWidth}
            height={layout.innerHeight}
            fill="transparent"
          />
        </g>
      </svg>
    {/if}
  </div>
  
  <style>
    .chart-container {
      position: relative;
      flex: 1;
      height: 100%;
      contain: layout style paint;
    }
  
    .svg-background, .canvas-layer, .svg-foreground {
      position: absolute;
      top: 0;
      left: 0;
    }
  
    .svg-background { z-index: 1; }
    .canvas-layer { z-index: 2; }
    .svg-foreground { z-index: 3; }
  
    .crosshair {
      cursor: crosshair;
    }
  </style>
</src/lib/components/charts/ChartContainer.svelte>

<src/lib/components/marks/scaleIntegration.svelte.js>
  // src/lib/runes/scaleIntegration.svelte.js
  export function createScaleIntegration(client, mapping, markId, chartContext) {
    $effect(() => {
      if (!client?.data || !chartContext) return;
      
      console.log('scale integarion addMark running!!!')
      // This replaces the scale logic currently in DataClient._updateScales()
      for (const [channel, field] of Object.entries(mapping)) {
        const scaleState = chartContext[`${channel}ScaleState`];
        if (scaleState && field) {
          const values = client.data.map(d => d[field]).filter(v => v != null);
          if (values.length > 0) {
            scaleState.addMark(values, markId);
          }
        }
      }
    });
  
    $effect(() => {
      return () => {
        // Cleanup - replaces logic currently in DataClient.destroy()
        for (const channel of Object.keys(mapping)) {
          const scaleState = chartContext[`${channel}ScaleState`];
          if (scaleState) {
            scaleState.removeMark(markId);
          }
        }
      };
    });
  }
</src/lib/components/marks/scaleIntegration.svelte.js>

<src/lib/components/marks/BaseMarkSetup.svelte.js>
  // src/lib/components/marks/BaseMarkSetup.svelte.js
  import { DataClient } from '$lib/data-clients/DataClient.svelte.js';
  import { SCALE_PRIORITIES } from '$lib/scales/ScalePriorities';
  import { Query, column as sqlColumn } from '@uwdata/mosaic-sql';
  
  /**
   * Generic controller that handles common mark lifecycle and state management.
   * This is the "General Manager" in the 4-layer architecture.
   * 
   * Uses DataClient for all query/data logic - doesn't reimplement anything!
   */
  export class BaseMarkSetup {
    // ✅ Reactive state using Svelte 5 runes
    client = $state(null);
    categoricalDataFetched = $state(false);
    
    // ✅ Configuration (non-reactive)
    #config;
    #coordinator;
    #chartContext;
    #markId;
  
    constructor(config, coordinator, chartContext) {
      this.#config = config;
      this.#coordinator = coordinator;
      this.#chartContext = chartContext;
      this.#markId = `${config.markType}-${crypto.randomUUID().slice(0, 8)}`;
      
      // Register scale type preferences
      this.#chartContext.registerScaleType(
        'x', 
        config.scaleRequirements.x.type, 
        config.scaleRequirements.x.priority, 
        this.#markId
      );
      this.#chartContext.registerScaleType(
        'y', 
        config.scaleRequirements.y.type, 
        config.scaleRequirements.y.priority, 
        this.#markId
      );
    }
  
    // ===== REACTIVE GETTERS =====
    
    get actualXScaleType() {
      return this.#chartContext.scaleTypes?.x || 'linear';
    }
  
    get actualYScaleType() {
      return this.#chartContext.scaleTypes?.y || 'linear';
    }
  
    get markId() {
      return this.#markId;
    }
  
    get chartContext() {
      return this.#chartContext;
    }
  
    // ===== SCALE COMPATIBILITY =====
    
    scaleCompatibilityError = $derived.by(() => {
      const { supportedScaleTypes, markType } = this.#config;
      const incompatibleX = this.actualXScaleType && !supportedScaleTypes.x.includes(this.actualXScaleType);
      const incompatibleY = this.actualYScaleType && !supportedScaleTypes.y.includes(this.actualYScaleType);
      
      if (incompatibleX || incompatibleY) {
        return `${markType}Mark cannot render with scale types: x=${this.actualXScaleType}, y=${this.actualYScaleType}`;
      }
      
      // Warn if different from preferred
      const { scaleRequirements } = this.#config;
      if (this.actualXScaleType !== scaleRequirements.x.type || this.actualYScaleType !== scaleRequirements.y.type) {
        console.warn(
          `${markType}Mark: Scale types resolved differently than preferred. ` +
          `Preferred: x=${scaleRequirements.x.type}, y=${scaleRequirements.y.type}. ` +
          `Actual: x=${this.actualXScaleType}, y=${this.actualYScaleType}. ` +
          `This might be due to other marks with higher priority requirements.`
        );
      }
      
      return null;
    });
  
    // ===== GPU READINESS =====
    
    gpuReady = $derived.by(() => {
      if (!this.#chartContext.scalesReady || !this.categoricalDataFetched) return false;
      
      const xReady = (this.actualXScaleType === 'band' || this.actualXScaleType === 'point') 
        ? this.#chartContext.xScaleState?.isReadyForGPU 
        : true;
        
      const yReady = (this.actualYScaleType === 'band' || this.actualYScaleType === 'point')
        ? this.#chartContext.yScaleState?.isReadyForGPU 
        : true;
        
      return xReady && yReady;
    });
  
    // ===== VIRTUAL METHOD FOR SPECIALIZATION =====
    
    /**
     * Get the select configuration for DataClient.
     * Base implementation: simple non-aggregated select.
     * Specialized controllers override this for complex queries (aggregations, etc.)
     * @returns {Object} Select configuration for DataClient
     */
    getSelectConfig() {
      const { x, y, color } = this.#config;
      
      return {
        x, 
        y, 
        ...(color && { color })
      };
    }
  
    // ===== CATEGORICAL DOMAIN FETCHING =====
    
    async fetchCategoricalDomains() {
      const { table, selection } = this.#config;
      if (!this.#coordinator || !table) return;
  
      const promises = [];
  
      // Fetch x-axis categories if needed
      if ((this.actualXScaleType === 'band' || this.actualXScaleType === 'point') && this.#config.x) {
        const distinctQuery = Query.from(table)
          .select({ x: sqlColumn(this.#config.x) })
          .distinct()
          .where(selection?.predicate?.(table) || []);
        
        promises.push(
          this.#coordinator.query(distinctQuery).then(result => {
            const categories = Array.from(result).map(row => row.x).filter(v => v != null);
            if (categories.length > 0) {
              this.#chartContext.xScaleState.addMark(categories, this.#markId);
            }
          })
        );
      }
  
      // Fetch y-axis categories if needed  
      if ((this.actualYScaleType === 'band' || this.actualYScaleType === 'point') && this.#config.y) {
        const distinctQuery = Query.from(table)
          .select({ y: sqlColumn(this.#config.y) })
          .distinct()
          .where(selection?.predicate?.(table) || []);
        
        promises.push(
          this.#coordinator.query(distinctQuery).then(result => {
            const categories = Array.from(result).map(row => row.y).filter(v => v != null);
            if (categories.length > 0) {
              this.#chartContext.yScaleState.addMark(categories, this.#markId);
            }
          })
        );
      }
  
      if (promises.length > 0) {
        await Promise.all(promises);
      }
      
      this.categoricalDataFetched = true;
    }
  
    // ===== CLIENT MANAGEMENT =====
    
    createClient() {
      if (this.client) {
        this.client.destroy();
        this.client = null;
      }
  
      const { table, selection, clientOptions } = this.#config;
  
      // Just use DataClient normally - let it handle all the query logic!
      this.client = new DataClient(table, selection, {
        select: this.getSelectConfig(), // This is where specialization happens
        dataFormat: 'arrow',
        name: this.#markId,
        ...clientOptions
      });
  
      if (this.#coordinator) {
        this.#coordinator.connect(this.client);
      }
    }
  
    // ===== LIFECYCLE METHODS =====
    
    async setup() {
      try {
        await this.fetchCategoricalDomains();
        this.createClient();
      } catch (error) {
        console.error(`Failed to setup ${this.#config.markType}Mark:`, error);
      }
    }
  
    async updateConfig(newConfig) {
      const needsReset = 
        newConfig.x !== this.#config.x ||
        newConfig.y !== this.#config.y ||
        newConfig.table !== this.#config.table ||
        newConfig.color !== this.#config.color;
  
      this.#config = { ...this.#config, ...newConfig };
  
      if (needsReset) {
        this.categoricalDataFetched = false;
        await this.setup();
      }
    }
  
    destroy() {
      this.#chartContext.unregisterScaleType('x', this.#markId);
      this.#chartContext.unregisterScaleType('y', this.#markId);
  
      if (this.client) {
        this.client.destroy();
        this.client = null;
      }
    }
  }
</src/lib/components/marks/BaseMarkSetup.svelte.js>

<src/lib/components/marks/MarkSetup.svelte.js>
  // src/lib/components/marks/MarkSetup.svelte.js
  import { DataClient } from '$lib/data-clients/DataClient.svelte.js';
  import { SCALE_PRIORITIES } from '$lib/scales/ScalePriorities';
  import { Query, column as sqlColumn } from '@uwdata/mosaic-sql';
  
  export class MarkSetup {
    // ✅ Reactive state using Svelte 5 runes
    client = $state(null);
    categoricalDataFetched = $state(false);
    
    // ✅ Configuration (non-reactive)
    #config;
    #coordinator;
    #chartContext;
    #markId;
  
    constructor(config, coordinator, chartContext) {
      this.#config = config;
      this.#coordinator = coordinator;
      this.#chartContext = chartContext;
      this.#markId = `${config.markType}-${crypto.randomUUID().slice(0, 8)}`;
      
      // Register scale type preferences
      this.#chartContext.registerScaleType(
        'x', 
        config.scaleRequirements.x.type, 
        config.scaleRequirements.x.priority, 
        this.#markId
      );
      this.#chartContext.registerScaleType(
        'y', 
        config.scaleRequirements.y.type, 
        config.scaleRequirements.y.priority, 
        this.#markId
      );
    }
  
    // ✅ Reactive getters using $derived
    get actualXScaleType() {
      return this.#chartContext.scaleTypes?.x || 'linear';
    }
  
    get actualYScaleType() {
      return this.#chartContext.scaleTypes?.y || 'linear';
    }
  
    get markId() {
      return this.#markId;
    }
  
    get chartContext() {
      return this.#chartContext;
    }
  
    // ✅ Scale compatibility validation
    scaleCompatibilityError = $derived.by(() => {
      const { supportedScaleTypes, markType } = this.#config;
      const incompatibleX = this.actualXScaleType && !supportedScaleTypes.x.includes(this.actualXScaleType);
      const incompatibleY = this.actualYScaleType && !supportedScaleTypes.y.includes(this.actualYScaleType);
      
      if (incompatibleX || incompatibleY) {
        return `${markType}Mark cannot render with scale types: x=${this.actualXScaleType}, y=${this.actualYScaleType}`;
      }
      
      // Warn if different from preferred
      const { scaleRequirements } = this.#config;
      if (this.actualXScaleType !== scaleRequirements.x.type || this.actualYScaleType !== scaleRequirements.y.type) {
        console.warn(
          `${markType}Mark: Scale types resolved differently than preferred. ` +
          `Preferred: x=${scaleRequirements.x.type}, y=${scaleRequirements.y.type}. ` +
          `Actual: x=${this.actualXScaleType}, y=${this.actualYScaleType}. ` +
          `This might be due to other marks with higher priority requirements.`
        );
      }
      
      return null;
    });
  
    // ✅ GPU readiness check
    gpuReady = $derived.by(() => {
      if (!this.#chartContext.scalesReady || !this.categoricalDataFetched) return false;
      
      const xReady = (this.actualXScaleType === 'band' || this.actualXScaleType === 'point') 
        ? this.#chartContext.xScaleState?.isReadyForGPU 
        : true;
        
      const yReady = (this.actualYScaleType === 'band' || this.actualYScaleType === 'point')
        ? this.#chartContext.yScaleState?.isReadyForGPU 
        : true;
        
      return xReady && yReady;
    });
  
    // ✅ Methods for lifecycle management
    async fetchCategoricalDomains() {
      const { table, selection, x, y } = this.#config;
      if (!this.#coordinator || !table) return;
  
      const promises = [];
  
      // Fetch x-axis categories if needed
      if ((this.actualXScaleType === 'band' || this.actualXScaleType === 'point') && x) {
        const distinctQuery = Query.from(table)
          .select({ x: sqlColumn(x) })
          .distinct()
          .where(selection?.predicate?.(table) || []);
        
        promises.push(
          this.#coordinator.query(distinctQuery).then(result => {
            const categories = Array.from(result).map(row => row.x).filter(v => v != null);
            if (categories.length > 0) {
              this.#chartContext.xScaleState.addMark(categories, this.#markId);
            }
          })
        );
      }
  
      // Fetch y-axis categories if needed  
      if ((this.actualYScaleType === 'band' || this.actualYScaleType === 'point') && y) {
        const distinctQuery = Query.from(table)
          .select({ y: sqlColumn(y) })
          .distinct()
          .where(selection?.predicate?.(table) || []);
        
        promises.push(
          this.#coordinator.query(distinctQuery).then(result => {
            const categories = Array.from(result).map(row => row.y).filter(v => v != null);
            if (categories.length > 0) {
              this.#chartContext.yScaleState.addMark(categories, this.#markId);
            }
          })
        );
      }
  
      if (promises.length > 0) {
        await Promise.all(promises);
      }
      
      this.categoricalDataFetched = true;
    }
  
    createClient() {
      if (this.client) {
        this.client.destroy();
        this.client = null;
      }
  
      const { table, selection, x, y, color, clientOptions } = this.#config;
  
      this.client = new DataClient(table, selection, {
        select: {
          x, 
          y, 
          ...(color && { color })
        },
        dataFormat: 'arrow',
        name: this.#markId,
        ...clientOptions
      });
  
      if (this.#coordinator) {
        this.#coordinator.connect(this.client);
      }
    }
  
    // ✅ Combined setup method
    async setup() {
      try {
        await this.fetchCategoricalDomains();
        this.createClient();
      } catch (error) {
        console.error(`Failed to setup ${this.#config.markType}Mark:`, error);
      }
    }
  
    // ✅ Handle prop changes
    async updateConfig(newConfig) {
      const needsReset = 
        newConfig.x !== this.#config.x ||
        newConfig.y !== this.#config.y ||
        newConfig.table !== this.#config.table ||
        newConfig.color !== this.#config.color;
  
      this.#config = { ...this.#config, ...newConfig };
  
      if (needsReset) {
        this.categoricalDataFetched = false;
        await this.setup();
      }
    }
  
    // ✅ Cleanup
    destroy() {
      this.#chartContext.unregisterScaleType('x', this.#markId);
      this.#chartContext.unregisterScaleType('y', this.#markId);
  
      if (this.client) {
        this.client.destroy();
        this.client = null;
      }
    }
  }
</src/lib/components/marks/MarkSetup.svelte.js>

<src/lib/components/marks/DotsMarkClass.svelte>
  <!-- src/lib/components/marks/DotsMark.svelte -->
  <script>
      import { getContext, onMount, onDestroy, untrack } from 'svelte';
      import { getScaleState } from '$lib/scales';
      import { SCALE_PRIORITIES } from '$lib/scales/ScalePriorities';
      import { BaseMarkSetup } from './BaseMarkSetup.svelte.js';
      import { Dots } from '$lib/components';
    
      // ===== PROPS (User Intent) =====
      let { 
        table, 
        selection, 
        x, 
        y, 
        color, 
        alpha = 0.5, 
        radius = 3,
        limit,
        filterNulls,
        preferredXScaleType = 'linear', 
        preferredYScaleType = 'linear',
      } = $props();
    
      // ===== CONTEXT =====
      const coordinator = getContext('mosaic-coordinator');
      const c = getContext('chart');
      
      // Get color scale for the color encoding
      let colorScale = $derived(getScaleState(color, 'ordinal'));
    
      // ===== CONTROLLER SETUP =====
      // For simple dots, we can use BaseMarkSetup directly!
      // It will use DataClient with a simple { x, y, color } select - no aggregation needed
      const markSetup = new BaseMarkSetup({
        table,
        selection,
        x,
        y,
        color,
        markType: 'dot',
        scaleRequirements: {
          x: { type: preferredXScaleType, priority: SCALE_PRIORITIES.PREFER },
          y: { type: preferredYScaleType, priority: SCALE_PRIORITIES.PREFER }
        },
        supportedScaleTypes: {
          x: ['linear', 'identity', 'band', 'point'],
          y: ['linear', 'identity', 'band', 'point']
        },
        clientOptions: {
          limit,
          ...(filterNulls && { filterNulls })
        }
      }, coordinator, c);
    
      // ===== LIFECYCLE MANAGEMENT =====
      onMount(() => {
        markSetup.setup();
      });
    
      // Handle prop changes - just update the controller
      $effect(() => {
        void x; void y; void color; void table;
        void c.scaleTypes; // React to scale type changes
        untrack(() => {
          markSetup.updateConfig({ table, x, y, color });
        });
      });
    
      onDestroy(() => {
        markSetup.destroy();
      });
    </script>
    
    <!-- ===== PURE VIEW LOGIC ===== -->
    {#if markSetup.scaleCompatibilityError}
      <text x={c.marginLeft ?? 20} y={c.marginTop ?? 20} fill="red" font-size="14">
        Scale Error: {markSetup.scaleCompatibilityError}
      </text>
    {:else if markSetup.client?.arrowTable && markSetup.gpuReady}
      <!-- Pass resolved scale types to the low-level renderer -->
      <Dots
        data={markSetup.client.arrowTable}
        dataVersion={markSetup.client.dataVersion}
        xScaleType={markSetup.actualXScaleType}
        yScaleType={markSetup.actualYScaleType}
        alpha={alpha}
        r={radius}
      />
    {:else if markSetup.client?.error}
      <text x={c.marginLeft ?? 20} y={c.marginTop ?? 20} fill="red" font-size="14">
        Error: {markSetup.client.error.message}
      </text>
    {:else if markSetup.client?.delayedLoading}
      <text x={c.marginLeft ?? 20} y={c.marginTop ?? 20} fill="currentColor" font-size="14">
        Loading...
      </text>
    {:else}
      <text x={c.marginLeft ?? 20} y={c.marginTop ?? 20} fill="currentColor" font-size="14">
        No data available
      </text>
    {/if}
</src/lib/components/marks/DotsMarkClass.svelte>

<src/lib/components/marks/DotsMark.svelte>
  <!-- src/lib/components/marks/DotsMark.svelte -->
  <script>
    import { DataClient } from '$lib/data-clients/DataClient.svelte.js';
    import { getContext, onMount, onDestroy, untrack } from 'svelte';
    import { getScaleState } from '$lib/scales';
    import { SCALE_PRIORITIES } from '$lib/scales/ScalePriorities';
    import { createScaleIntegration } from './scaleIntegration.svelte';
    import { Dots } from '$lib/components';
  
    let { 
      table, 
      selection, 
      x, 
      y, 
      color, 
      alpha = 0.5, 
      radius = 3,
      limit,
      filterNulls,
      preferredXScaleType = 'linear', 
      preferredYScaleType = 'linear',
    } = $props();
  
    const coordinator = getContext('mosaic-coordinator');
    const c = getContext('chart');
    const markId = `dot-${crypto.randomUUID().slice(0, 8)}`;
  
    // Get color scale for the color encoding
    let colorScale = $derived(getScaleState(color, 'ordinal'));
    let client = $state(null);
  
    // Register scale type preferences with chart container
    c.registerScaleType('x', preferredXScaleType, SCALE_PRIORITIES.PREFER, markId);
    c.registerScaleType('y', preferredYScaleType, SCALE_PRIORITIES.PREFER, markId);
  
    // Get the actual resolved scale types
    let actualXScaleType = $derived(c.scaleTypes?.x || 'linear');
    let actualYScaleType = $derived(c.scaleTypes?.y || 'linear');
  
    // Validation: Check if dots can work with the resolved scale types
    let scaleCompatibilityError = $derived.by(() => {
      const incompatibleX = actualXScaleType && !['linear', 'identity', 'band', 'point'].includes(actualXScaleType);
      const incompatibleY = actualYScaleType && !['linear', 'identity', 'band', 'point'].includes(actualYScaleType);
      
      if (incompatibleX || incompatibleY) {
        return `DotsMark cannot render with scale types: x=${actualXScaleType}, y=${actualYScaleType}`;
      }
      
      // Warn if we got something different from what we preferred
      if (actualXScaleType !== preferredXScaleType || actualYScaleType !== preferredYScaleType) {
        console.warn(
          `DotsMark: Scale types resolved differently than preferred. ` +
          `Preferred: x=${preferredXScaleType}, y=${preferredYScaleType}. ` +
          `Actual: x=${actualXScaleType}, y=${actualYScaleType}. ` +
          `This might be due to other marks with higher priority requirements.`
        );
      }
      
      return null;
    });
  
  
    function createClient() {
      if (client) {
        client.destroy();
        client = null;
      }
  
      client = new DataClient(table, selection, {
        select: {
          x, 
          y, 
          ...(color && { color })
        },
        limit,
        ...(filterNulls && { filterNulls }),
        dataFormat: 'arrow',
        name: markId
      });
  
      if (coordinator) {
        coordinator.connect(client);
      }
  
      // createScaleIntegration(client, { x, y }, markId, c);
    }
  
  
    onMount(() => {
      createClient();
    });
  
    $effect(() => {
      void x; void y; void color; void table;
      untrack(() => {
        if (client) createClient();
      })
    });
  
    onDestroy(() => {
      c.unregisterScaleType('x', markId);
      c.unregisterScaleType('y', markId);
  
      if (client) {
        client.destroy();
      }
    });
  
    let gpuReady = $derived.by(() => {
      if (!c.scalesReady) return false;
      
      // Check if any band/point scales are ready for GPU processing
      const xReady = (c.scaleTypes?.x === 'band' || c.scaleTypes?.x === 'point') 
        ? c.xScaleState?.isReadyForGPU 
        : true;
        
      const yReady = (c.scaleTypes?.y === 'band' || c.scaleTypes?.y === 'point')
        ? c.yScaleState?.isReadyForGPU 
        : true;
        
      return xReady && yReady;
    });
  
    $inspect('gpuReady dots mark', gpuReady)
  
  </script>
  
  {#if scaleCompatibilityError}
    <text x={c.marginLeft ?? 20} y={c.marginTop ?? 20} fill="red" font-size="14">
      Scale Error: {scaleCompatibilityError}
    </text>
  {:else if client?.arrowTable && gpuReady}
    <!-- Pass the ACTUAL resolved scale types, not the requested ones -->
    <Dots
      data={client.arrowTable}
      dataVersion={client.dataVersion}
      xScaleType={actualXScaleType}
      yScaleType={actualYScaleType}
      alpha={alpha}
      r={radius}
    />
  {:else if client?.error}
    <text x={c.marginLeft ?? 20} y={c.marginTop ?? 20} fill="red" font-size="14">
      Error: {client.error.message}
    </text>
  {:else if client?.delayedLoading}
    <text x={c.marginLeft ?? 20} y={c.marginTop ?? 20} fill="currentColor" font-size="14">
      Loading...
    </text>
  {:else}
    <text x={c.marginLeft ?? 20} y={c.marginTop ?? 20} fill="currentColor" font-size="14">
      No data available
    </text>
  {/if}
</src/lib/components/marks/DotsMark.svelte>
